    17→def log_ram_usage(label: str, task_id: str = "unknown") -> dict:
    18→    """
    19→    Log current RAM usage with a descriptive label.
    20→    Returns dict with RAM metrics for programmatic use.
    21→    """
    22→    if not _PSUTIL_AVAILABLE:
    23→        return {"available": False}
    24→
    25→    try:
    26→        process = psutil.Process(os.getpid())
    27→        mem_info = process.memory_info()
    28→        rss_mb = mem_info.rss / BYTES_PER_MB
    29→        rss_gb = mem_info.rss / BYTES_PER_GB
    30→
    31→        # Get system-wide memory stats
    32→        sys_mem = psutil.virtual_memory()
    33→        sys_total_gb = sys_mem.total / BYTES_PER_GB
    34→        sys_available_gb = sys_mem.available / BYTES_PER_GB
    35→        sys_used_percent = sys_mem.percent
    36→
    37→        headless_logger.info(
    38→            f"[RAM] {label}: Process={rss_mb:.0f}MB ({rss_gb:.2f}GB) | "
    39→            f"System={sys_used_percent:.1f}% used, {sys_available_gb:.1f}GB/{sys_total_gb:.1f}GB available",
    40→            task_id=task_id
    41→        )
    42→
    43→        return {
    44→            "available": True,
    45→            "process_rss_mb": rss_mb,
    46→            "process_rss_gb": rss_gb,
    47→            "system_total_gb": sys_total_gb,
    48→            "system_available_gb": sys_available_gb,
    49→            "system_used_percent": sys_used_percent
    50→        }
    51→
    52→    except (ProcessLookupError, OSError) as e:
    53→        headless_logger.warning(f"[RAM] Failed to get RAM usage: {e}", task_id=task_id)
    54→        return {"available": False, "error": str(e)}

    56→def cleanup_generated_files(output_location: str, task_id: str = "unknown", debug_mode: bool = False) -> None:
    57→    """
    58→    Delete generated files after successful task completion unless in debug mode.
    59→    This includes the main output file/directory and any temporary files that may have been created.
    60→
    61→    Args:
    62→        output_location: Path to the generated file or directory to clean up
    63→        task_id: Task ID for logging purposes
    64→        debug_mode: Whether debug mode is enabled (skips cleanup if True)
    65→    """
    66→    if debug_mode:
    67→        headless_logger.debug(f"Debug mode enabled - skipping file cleanup for {output_location}", task_id=task_id)
    68→        return
    69→
    70→    if not output_location:
    71→        return
    72→
    73→    try:
    74→        file_path = Path(output_location)
    75→
    76→        # Clean up main output file/directory
    77→        if file_path.exists() and file_path.is_file():
    78→            file_size = file_path.stat().st_size
    79→            file_path.unlink()
    80→            headless_logger.debug(f"Cleaned up generated file: {output_location} ({file_size:,} bytes)", task_id=task_id)
    81→        elif file_path.exists() and file_path.is_dir():
    82→            dir_size = sum(f.stat().st_size for f in file_path.rglob('*') if f.is_file())
    83→            shutil.rmtree(file_path)
    84→            headless_logger.debug(f"Cleaned up generated directory: {output_location} ({dir_size:,} bytes)", task_id=task_id)
    85→        else:
    86→            headless_logger.debug(f"File cleanup skipped - path not found: {output_location}", task_id=task_id)
    87→
    88→    except OSError as e:
    89→        headless_logger.warning(f"Failed to cleanup generated file {output_location}: {e}", task_id=task_id)
    90→
    91→

   220→def execute_generation_impl(queue: Any, task: Any, worker_name: str) -> str:
   221→    """
   222→    Execute the actual generation using headless_wgp.py.
   223→
   224→    This delegates to the orchestrator while providing progress tracking
   225→    and integration with our queue system. Enhanced to support video guides,
   226→    masks, image references, and other advanced features.
   227→    """
   228→    # Ensure orchestrator is initialized before generation
   229→    queue._ensure_orchestrator()
   230→
   231→    queue.logger.info(f"{worker_name} executing generation for task {task.id} (model: {task.model})")
   232→
   233→    # Convert task parameters to WanOrchestrator format
   234→    wgp_params = queue._convert_to_wgp_task(task)
   235→
   236→    # Remove model and prompt from params since they're passed separately to avoid duplication
   237→    generation_params = {k: v for k, v in wgp_params.items() if k not in ("model", "prompt")}
   238→
   239→    # DEBUG: Log all parameter keys to verify _parsed_phase_config is present
   240→    queue.logger.info(f"[PHASE_CONFIG_DEBUG] Task {task.id}: generation_params keys: {list(generation_params.keys())}")
   241→
   242→    # CRITICAL: Apply phase_config patches NOW in the worker thread where wgp is imported
   243→    # Acquire lock to prevent concurrent tasks from corrupting shared wgp globals
   244→    with _wgp_patch_lock:
   245→        return _execute_generation_with_patches(queue, task, worker_name, generation_params)
   246→
   247→
   248→def _execute_generation_with_patches(
   249→    queue: Any,
   250→    task: Any,
   251→    worker_name: str,
   252→    generation_params: dict,
   253→):
   254→    """Run generation with wgp global patching, under _wgp_patch_lock."""
   255→    # Store patch info for cleanup in finally block
   256→    _patch_applied = False
   257→    _parsed_phase_config_for_restore = None
   258→    _model_name_for_restore = None
   259→
   260→    if "_parsed_phase_config" in generation_params and "_phase_config_model_name" in generation_params:
   261→        parsed_phase_config = generation_params.pop("_parsed_phase_config")
   262→        model_name = generation_params.pop("_phase_config_model_name")
   263→
   264→        # Save for restoration
   265→        _parsed_phase_config_for_restore = parsed_phase_config
   266→        _model_name_for_restore = model_name
   267→
   268→        queue.logger.info(f"[PHASE_CONFIG] Applying model patch in GenerationWorker for '{model_name}'")
   269→
   270→        from source.core.params.phase_config import apply_phase_config_patch
   271→        apply_phase_config_patch(parsed_phase_config, model_name, task.id)
   272→        _patch_applied = True
   273→
   274→    # Handle svi2pro: This is a model_def property, not a generate() parameter
   275→    # We need to patch it into BOTH wgp.models_def AND wan_model.model_def
   276→    # because the model captures model_def at load time, not at generation time
   277→    _svi2pro_original = None

   279→    _wan_model_patched = False

   291→
   292→            # Patch 2: wan_model.model_def DIRECTLY (the actual object used during generation)

1→"""
     2→Queue lifecycle management extracted from HeadlessTaskQueue.
     3→
     4→Contains start, stop, and submit_task logic.  Every public function takes the
     5→``HeadlessTaskQueue`` instance (aliased *queue*) as its first argument.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from __future__ import annotations
     9→
    10→import threading
    11→import time
    12→import traceback
    13→from typing import Any
    14→
    15→from source.task_handlers.queue.task_processor import worker_loop, _monitor_loop
    16→
    17→
    18→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    19→# Start
    20→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    21→
    22→def start_queue(queue: Any, preload_model=None):
    23→    """
    24→    Start the task queue processing service.
    25→
    26→    Args:
    27→        queue: The HeadlessTaskQueue instance
    28→        preload_model: Optional model to pre-load before processing tasks.
    29→                      If specified, the model will be loaded immediately after
    30→                      workers start, making the first task much faster.
    31→                      Example: "wan_2_2_vace_lightning_baseline_2_2_2"
    32→    """
    33→    if queue.running:
    34→        queue.logger.warning("Queue already running")
    35→        return
    36→
    37→    queue.running = True
    38→    queue.shutdown_event.clear()
    39→
    40→    # Start worker threads
    41→    for i in range(queue.max_workers):
    42→        worker = threading.Thread(
    43→            target=worker_loop,
    44→            args=(queue,),
    45→            name=f"GenerationWorker-{i}",
    46→            daemon=True
    47→        )
    48→        worker.start()
    49→        queue.worker_threads.append(worker)
    50→
    51→    # Start monitoring thread
    52→    monitor = threading.Thread(
    53→        target=_monitor_loop,
    54→        args=(queue,),
    55→        name="QueueMonitor",
    56→        daemon=True
    57→    )
    58→    monitor.start()
    59→    queue.worker_threads.append(monitor)
    60→
    61→    queue.logger.info(f"Task queue started with {queue.max_workers} workers")
    62→
    63→    # Pre-load model if specified
    64→    if preload_model:
    65→        queue.logger.info(f"Pre-loading model: {preload_model}")
    66→        try:
    67→            # Initialize orchestrator and load model in background
    68→            queue._ensure_orchestrator()
    69→            if queue.orchestrator is None:
    70→                raise RuntimeError("Orchestrator initialization failed during preload")
    71→            queue.orchestrator.load_model(preload_model)
    72→            queue.current_model = preload_model
    73→            queue.logger.info(f"Model {preload_model} pre-loaded successfully")
    74→        except (RuntimeError, ValueError, OSError) as e:
    75→            # Log the full error with traceback
    76→            queue.logger.error(f"FATAL: Failed to pre-load model {preload_model}: {e}\n{traceback.format_exc()}")
    77→
    78→            # If orchestrator failed to initialize, this is fatal - worker cannot function
    79→            if queue.orchestrator is None:
    80→                queue.logger.error("Orchestrator failed to initialize - worker cannot process tasks. Exiting.")
    81→                raise RuntimeError(f"Orchestrator initialization failed during preload: {e}") from e
    82→            else:
    83→                # Orchestrator is OK but model load failed - this is recoverable
    84→                queue.logger.warning(f"Model {preload_model} failed to load, but orchestrator is ready. Worker will continue.")
    85→
    86→
    87→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    88→# Stop
    89→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    90→
    91→def stop_queue(queue: Any, timeout: float = 30.0):
    92→    """Stop the task queue processing service."""
    93→    if not queue.running:
    94→        return
    95→
    96→    queue.logger.info("Shutting down task queue...")
    97→    queue.running = False
    98→    queue.shutdown_event.set()
    99→
   100→    # Wait for workers to finish
   101→    for worker in queue.worker_threads:
   102→        worker.join(timeout=timeout)
   103→
   104→    # Save queue state (integrate with wgp.py's save system)
   105→    queue._save_queue_state()
   106→
   107→    # Optionally unload model to free VRAM
   108→    # queue._cleanup_models()
   109→
   110→    queue.logger.info("Task queue shutdown complete")
   111→
   112→
   113→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   114→# Submit
   115→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   116→
   117→def submit_task_impl(queue: Any, task: Any) -> str:
   118→    """
   119→    Submit a new generation task to the queue.
   120→
   121→    Args:
   122→        queue: The HeadlessTaskQueue instance
   123→        task: Generation task to process
   124→
   125→    Returns:
   126→        Task ID for tracking
   127→    """
   128→    with queue.queue_lock:
   129→        # Pre-validate task conversion (actual conversion happens in _process_task)
   130→        queue._convert_to_wgp_task(task)
   131→
   132→        # Add to queue with priority (higher priority = processed first)
   133→        queue.task_queue.put((-task.priority, time.time(), task))
   134→        queue.task_history[task.id] = task
   135→        queue.stats["tasks_submitted"] += 1
   136→
   137→        queue.logger.info(f"Task submitted: {task.id} (model: {task.model}, priority: {task.priority})")
   138→        return task.id
   139→

     5→A persistent service that maintains model state and processes generation tasks

    54→def setup_wgp_path(wan_dir: str):
    55→    """Setup WanGP path and imports."""
    56→    wan_dir = os.path.abspath(wan_dir)
    57→    if wan_dir not in sys.path:

    75→

    79→
    80→@dataclass

    92→class HeadlessTaskQueue(WgpInitMixin):
    93→    """
    94→    Main task queue manager that integrates with wgp.py's existing queue system.
    95→
    96→    This class leverages wgp.py's built-in task management and state persistence
    97→    while providing a clean API for headless operation.
    98→
    99→    Mixins:
   100→        WgpInitMixin: CUDA warmup and lazy WanOrchestrator initialization
   101→    """
   102→    
   103→    def __init__(self, wan_dir: str, max_workers: int = 1, debug_mode: bool = False, main_output_dir: Optional[str] = None):
   104→        """
   105→        Initialize the headless task queue.
   106→
   107→        Args:
   108→            wan_dir: Path to WanGP directory
   109→            max_workers: Number of concurrent generation workers (recommend 1 for GPU)
   110→            debug_mode: Enable verbose debug logging (should match worker's --debug flag)
   111→            main_output_dir: Optional path for output directory. If not provided, defaults to
   112→                           'outputs' directory next to wan_dir (preserves backwards compatibility)
   113→        """
   114→        self.wan_dir = setup_wgp_path(wan_dir)
   115→        self.max_workers = max_workers
   116→        self.main_output_dir = main_output_dir
   117→        self.running = False
   118→        self.start_time = time.time()
   119→        self.debug_mode = debug_mode  # Now controlled by caller
   120→        
   121→        # Import wgp after path setup (protect sys.argv to prevent argument conflicts)
   122→        _saved_argv = sys.argv[:]
   123→        sys.argv = ["headless_wgp.py"]
   124→        # Headless stubs to avoid optional UI deps (tkinter/matanyone) during import
   125→        try:
   126→            import types
   127→            # Stub tkinter if not available
   128→            if 'tkinter' not in sys.modules:
   129→                sys.modules['tkinter'] = types.ModuleType('tkinter')
   130→            # Stub preprocessing.matanyone.app with minimal interface
   131→            dummy_pkg = types.ModuleType('preprocessing')
   132→            dummy_matanyone = types.ModuleType('preprocessing.matanyone')
   133→            dummy_app = types.ModuleType('preprocessing.matanyone.app')
   134→            def _noop_handler():
   135→                class _Dummy:
   136→                    def __getattr__(self, _):
   137→                        return None
   138→                return _Dummy()
   139→            dummy_app.get_vmc_event_handler = _noop_handler  # type: ignore
   140→            sys.modules['preprocessing'] = dummy_pkg
   141→            sys.modules['preprocessing.matanyone'] = dummy_matanyone
   142→            sys.modules['preprocessing.matanyone.app'] = dummy_app
   143→        except (TypeError, AttributeError, ImportError) as e:
   144→            logging.getLogger('HeadlessQueue').debug(f"Failed to set up headless UI stubs: {e}")
   145→        # Don't import wgp during initialization to avoid CUDA/argparse conflicts
   146→        # wgp will be imported lazily when needed (e.g., in _apply_sampler_cfg_preset)
   147→        # This allows the queue to initialize even if CUDA isn't ready yet
   148→        self.wgp = None

   463→def worker_loop(queue: Any):
   464→    """Main worker loop for processing tasks."""
   465→    import threading
   466→    worker_name = threading.current_thread().name
   467→    queue.logger.info(f"{worker_name} started")
   468→
   469→    while queue.running and not queue.shutdown_event.is_set():
   470→        try:
   471→            # Get next task (blocks with timeout)
   472→            try:
   473→                priority, timestamp, task = queue.task_queue.get(timeout=1.0)
   474→            except queue_mod.Empty:
   475→                continue
   476→
   477→            # Process the task
   478→            process_task_impl(queue, task, worker_name)
   479→
   480→        except (RuntimeError, ValueError, OSError) as e:

   482→            time.sleep(1.0)
   483→

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Unified Debug Tool
==================

One tool to investigate tasks, workers, and system health.
Uses system_logs as the primary data source.

Usage:
    debug.py task <task_id>             # Investigate specific task
    debug.py worker <worker_id>         # Investigate specific worker
    debug.py tasks                      # Analyze recent tasks
    debug.py workers                    # List recent workers
    debug.py health                     # System health check
    debug.py orchestrator               # Orchestrator status

Options:
    --json                              # Output as JSON
    --hours N                           # Time window in hours
    --limit N                           # Limit results
    --logs-only                         # Show only logs timeline
    --debug                             # Show debug info on errors
"""

import sys
import argparse
from pathlib import Path

# Add project root to path so `import debug` works.
project_root = Path(__file__).parent.parent
sys.path.insert(0, str(project_root))

from debug.client import DebugClient


def create_parser() -> argparse.ArgumentParser:
    """Create argument parser."""
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        description="Unified debugging tool for investigating tasks, workers, and system health",
        formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter,
        epilog=__doc__,
    )

    subparsers = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command", help="Command to run", required=True)

    # Task command
    task_parser = subparsers.add_parser("task", help="Investigate specific task")
    task_parser.add_argument("task_id", help="Task ID to investigate")
    task_parser.add_argument("--json", action="store_true", help="Output as JSON")
    task_parser.add_argument("--logs-only", action="store_true", help="Show only logs timeline")
    task_parser.add_argument("--debug", action="store_true", help="Show debug info on errors")

    # Worker command
    worker_parser = subparsers.add_parser("worker", help="Investigate specific worker")
    worker_parser.add_argument("worker_id", help="Worker ID to investigate")
    worker_parser.add_argument("--hours", type=int, default=24, help="Hours of history (default: 24)")
    worker_parser.add_argument("--json", action="store_true", help="Output as JSON")
    worker_parser.add_argument("--logs-only", action="store_true", help="Show only logs timeline")
    worker_parser.add_argument("--startup", action="store_true", help="Show startup logs only")
    worker_parser.add_argument("--check-logging", action="store_true", help="Check if worker is logging")
    worker_parser.add_argument("--check-disk", action="store_true", help="Check disk space via SSH")
    worker_parser.add_argument("--debug", action="store_true", help="Show debug info on errors")

    # Tasks command
    tasks_parser = subparsers.add_parser("tasks", help="Analyze recent tasks")
    tasks_parser.add_argument("--limit", type=int, default=50, help="Number of tasks (default: 50)")
    tasks_parser.add_argument("--status", help="Filter by status")
    tasks_parser.add_argument("--type", help="Filter by task type")
    tasks_parser.add_argument("--worker", help="Filter by worker ID")
    tasks_parser.add_argument("--hours", type=int, help="Filter by hours")
    tasks_parser.add_argument("--json", action="store_true", help="Output as JSON")
    tasks_parser.add_argument("--debug", action="store_true", help="Show debug info on errors")

    # Workers command
    workers_parser = subparsers.add_parser("workers", help="List recent workers")
    workers_parser.add_argument("--hours", type=int, default=2, help="Hours of history (default: 2)")
    workers_parser.add_argument("--detailed", action="store_true", help="Show detailed analysis")
    workers_parser.add_argument("--json", action="store_true", help="Output as JSON")
    workers_parser.add_argument("--debug", action="store_true", help="Show debug info on errors")

    22→def add_task_to_db(task_payload: dict, task_type_str: str, dependant_on: str | list[str] | None = None, db_path: str | None = None) -> str:
    23→    """
    24→    Adds a new task to the Supabase database via Edge Function.
    25→
    26→    Args:
    27→        task_payload: Task parameters dictionary
    28→        task_type_str: Type of task being created
    29→        dependant_on: Optional dependency - single task ID string or list of task IDs.
    30→                      When list is provided, task will only run when ALL dependencies complete.
    31→        db_path: Ignored (kept for API compatibility)
    32→
    33→    Returns:
    34→        str: The database row ID (UUID) assigned to the task
    35→    """
    36→    # Generate a new UUID for the database row ID
    37→    import uuid
    38→    actual_db_row_id = str(uuid.uuid4())
    39→
    40→    # Sanitize payload and get project_id
    41→    params_for_db = task_payload.copy()
    42→    params_for_db.pop("task_type", None)  # Ensure task_type is not duplicated in params
    43→    project_id = task_payload.get("project_id", "default_project_id")
    44→
    45→    # Build Edge URL
    46→    edge_url = (
    47→        _cfg.SUPABASE_EDGE_CREATE_TASK_URL
    48→    ) or os.getenv("SUPABASE_EDGE_CREATE_TASK_URL") or (
    49→        f"{_cfg.SUPABASE_URL.rstrip('/')}/functions/v1/create-task" if _cfg.SUPABASE_URL else None
    50→    )
    51→
    52→    if not edge_url:
    53→        raise ValueError("Edge Function URL for create-task is not configured")
    54→
    55→    headers = {"Content-Type": "application/json"}
    56→    if _cfg.SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN:
    57→        headers["Authorization"] = f"Bearer {_cfg.SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN}"
    58→
    59→    # Normalize dependant_on to list format for consistency
    60→    # Edge Function expects: null, or JSON array of UUIDs
    61→    dependant_on_list: list[str] | None = None
    62→    if dependant_on:
    63→        if isinstance(dependant_on, str):
    64→            dependant_on_list = [dependant_on]
    65→        else:
    66→            dependant_on_list = list(dependant_on)
    67→
    68→    # Defensive check: if dependencies are specified, ensure they all exist before enqueueing
    69→    if dependant_on_list:
    70→        max_retries = 3
    71→        retry_delay = 0.5
    72→
    73→        for dep_id in dependant_on_list:
    74→            for attempt in range(max_retries):
    75→                try:
    76→                    if _cfg.SUPABASE_CLIENT:
    77→                        resp_exist = _cfg.SUPABASE_CLIENT.table(_cfg.PG_TABLE_NAME).select("id").eq("id", dep_id).single().execute()
    78→                        if not getattr(resp_exist, "data", None):
    79→                            headless_logger.debug(f"[ERROR][DEBUG_DEPENDENCY_CHAIN] dependant_on not found: {dep_id}. Refusing to create task of type {task_type_str} with broken dependency.")
    80→                            raise RuntimeError(f"dependant_on {dep_id} not found")

    27→    EDGE_FAIL_PREFIX,

    31→def _extract_video_thumbnail(video_path: Path, task_id_str: str) -> Path | None:
    32→    """Extract a thumbnail from a video file.
    33→
    34→    Checks for an existing poster (.jpg alongside the video) first.
    35→    Falls back to extracting the first frame via OpenCV.
    36→
    37→    Returns:
    38→        Path to the thumbnail file (caller must clean up temp files), or None.
    39→        If the returned path equals ``video_path.with_suffix('.jpg')``, it is
    40→        an existing file and should NOT be deleted by the caller.
    41→    """
    42→    import tempfile
    43→    try:
    44→        from ...utils import save_frame_from_video
    45→        import cv2
    46→    except ImportError:
    47→        _log_thumbnail("Cannot import cv2/save_frame_from_video for thumbnail extraction",
    48→                        level="warning", task_id=task_id_str)
    49→        return None
    50→
    51→    # Check for existing poster alongside the video
    52→    existing_poster_path = video_path.with_suffix('.jpg')
    53→    if existing_poster_path.exists():
    54→        _log_thumbnail(f"Found existing poster: {existing_poster_path.name}", level="info", task_id=task_id_str)
    55→        return existing_poster_path
    56→
    57→    # Extract first frame from video
    58→    _log_thumbnail(f"Extracting first frame from video {video_path.name}", task_id=task_id_str)
    59→    with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(suffix='.jpg', delete=False) as temp_frame:
    60→        temp_frame_path = Path(temp_frame.name)
    61→
    62→    cap = cv2.VideoCapture(str(video_path))
    63→    if not cap.isOpened():
    64→        _log_thumbnail(f"Could not open video with cv2: {video_path}", level="warning", task_id=task_id_str)
    65→        temp_frame_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
    66→        return None
    67→
    68→    width = int(cap.get(cv2.CAP_PROP_FRAME_WIDTH))
    69→    height = int(cap.get(cv2.CAP_PROP_FRAME_HEIGHT))
    70→    cap.release()
    71→
    72→    if save_frame_from_video(video_path, 0, temp_frame_path, (width, height)):
    73→        _log_thumbnail("First frame extracted successfully", level="info", task_id=task_id_str)
    74→        return temp_frame_path
    75→
    76→    _log_thumbnail(f"save_frame_from_video failed for {video_path.name}", level="warning", task_id=task_id_str)
    77→    temp_frame_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
    78→    return None
    79→
    80→def _mark_task_failed_via_edge_function(task_id_str: str, error_message: str):
    81→    """Mark a task as failed using the update-task-status Edge Function (with retry)."""
    82→    edge_url = (
    83→        os.getenv("SUPABASE_EDGE_UPDATE_TASK_URL")
    84→        or (f"{_cfg.SUPABASE_URL.rstrip('/')}/functions/v1/update-task-status" if _cfg.SUPABASE_URL else None)
    85→    )
    86→
    87→    if not edge_url:
    88→        headless_logger.error(f"No update-task-status edge function URL available for marking task {task_id_str} as failed", task_id=task_id_str)
    89→        return
    90→
    91→    headers = {"Content-Type": "application/json"}
    92→    if _cfg.SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN:
    93→        headers["Authorization"] = f"Bearer {_cfg.SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN}"
    94→
    95→    payload = {
    96→        "task_id": task_id_str,
    97→        "status": STATUS_FAILED,
    98→        "output_location": error_message
    99→    }
   100→

    67→def move_wgp_output_to_task_type_dir(output_path: str, task_type: str, task_id: str, main_output_dir_base: Path) -> str:
    68→    """
    69→    Move WGP-generated output from base directory to task-type subdirectory.
    70→
    71→    This function handles post-processing of WGP outputs to organize them by task_type.
    72→    WGP generates all files to a globally-configured base directory, so we move them
    73→    to task-type subdirectories after generation completes.
    74→
    75→    Args:
    76→        output_path: Path to the WGP-generated file (in base directory)
    77→        task_type: Task type (e.g., "vace", "flux", "t2v")
    78→        task_id: Task ID
    79→        main_output_dir_base: Base output directory
    80→
    81→    Returns:
    82→        New path if file was moved, original path otherwise
    83→    """
    84→    # Only process WGP task types
    85→    from source.task_handlers.tasks.task_types import WGP_TASK_TYPES
    86→    if task_type not in WGP_TASK_TYPES:
    87→        return output_path
    88→
    89→    try:
    90→        output_file = Path(output_path)
    91→
    92→        # Check if file exists
    93→        if not output_file.exists():
    94→            headless_logger.debug(f"Output file doesn't exist, skipping move: {output_path}", task_id=task_id)
    95→            return output_path
    96→
    97→        # Check if file is in base directory (not already in a subdirectory)
    98→        # If the parent directory is main_output_dir_base, it's in base directory
    99→        if output_file.parent.resolve() != main_output_dir_base.resolve():
   100→            headless_logger.debug(f"Output already in subdirectory, skipping move: {output_path}", task_id=task_id)
   101→            return output_path
   102→
   103→        # Generate new path in task-type subdirectory
   104→        filename = output_file.name
   105→        new_path, _ = prepare_output_path(
   106→            task_id=task_id,
   107→            filename=filename,
   108→            main_output_dir_base=main_output_dir_base,
   109→            task_type=task_type
   110→        )
   111→
   112→        # Move the file
   113→        headless_logger.info(
   114→            f"Moving WGP output to task-type directory: {output_file} → {new_path} (task_type={task_type})",
   115→            task_id=task_id
   116→        )
   117→
   118→        # Ensure destination directory exists
   119→        new_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   120→
   121→        # Move the file
   122→        shutil.move(str(output_file), str(new_path))
   123→
   124→        headless_logger.success(f"Moved WGP output to {new_path}", task_id=task_id)
   125→        return str(new_path)
   126→
   127→    except (OSError, shutil.Error, ValueError) as e:
   128→        headless_logger.error(f"Failed to move WGP output to task-type directory: {e}", task_id=task_id, exc_info=True)
   129→        # Return original path if move fails
   130→        return output_path

   203→def parse_args():

   205→    parser.add_argument("--main-output-dir", type=str, default="./outputs")

## Comprehensive Analysis: Travel Segment Generation Timeouts

I've thoroughly explored the codebase and identified the cause of the ~25-minute timeout for `individual_travel_segment` tasks. Here's my complete findings:

---

### 1. TIMEOUT MECHANISM

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py`, lines 1047-1094

The timeout is **hardcoded at 30 minutes (1800 seconds)**:

```python
max_wait_time = 1800  # 30 minutes
wait_interval = 2
elapsed_time = 0

while elapsed_time < max_wait_time:
    status = task_queue.get_task_status(task_id)
    if status is None: return False, f"Travel segment {task_id}: Task status became None"
    
    if status.status == "completed":
        # ... chaining logic ...
        return True, final_chained_path
    elif status.status == "failed":
        return False, f"Travel segment {task_id}: Generation failed: {status.error_message}"
    
    time.sleep(wait_interval)
    elapsed_time += wait_interval

return False, f"Travel segment {task_id}: Generation timeout"
```

**Key points:**
- Polls every 2 seconds
- After 1800 seconds (~30 min), returns `(False, "Travel segment {task_id}: Generation timeout")`
- This error message becomes the `output_location` in the DB (see worker.py line 478)

---

### 2. THE FAILED TASKS ANALYSIS

Your 3 failed tasks all ran for ~25 minutes before hitting the timeout. The facts:

| Aspect | Value | Significance |
|--------|-------|--------------|
| **Task Type** | `individual_travel_segment` | Standalone mode (no orchestrator) |
| **Model** | `wan_2_2_i2v_lightning_baseline_2_2_2` | LTX2 i2v, lightning=fast but requires specific frame counts |
| **Resolution** | 670×670 | **NON-STANDARD** - not 16:9, not standard grid size |
| **Frames** | 81 | **4N+1 format** (4×20 + 1 = 81) ✓ correct |
| **Inference Steps** | 6 | **2 phases of 3 steps** (lightning model uses phase_config) |
| **Generation Status** | `generation_created: false` | **Output was NEVER generated** |
| **Runtime** | ~25 minutes | **Suggests hang during generation, not timeout detection failure** |

---

### 3. THE ACTUAL PROBLEM: HUNG GENERATION, NOT TIMEOUT LOGIC

The timeout logic itself is working correctly. The real issue is that **WGP generation hung/stalled** for ~25 minutes before timing out.

**Why this matters:**
- The task was submitted to `task_queue.submit_task()` (line 1045)
- Then we poll `task_queue.get_task_status(task_id)` every 2 seconds for 30 minutes
- **After 25 minutes, the generation hadn't completed or failed**
- After 30 minutes, timeout returned the error message

**Where generation happens:**
`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/queue/task_processor.py`
- `process_task_impl()` (line 42) → actually runs the generation
- `execute_generation_impl()` (line 220) → delegates to WGP/orchestrator
- Worker loop continuously polls queue (line 463: `worker_loop()`) and processes tasks

---

### 4. UNLIKELY CAUSES (Ruled Out)

✓ **Timeout value is not the issue** — 30 minutes is reasonable for video generation  
✓ **Timeout detection logic is not the issue** — correctly polls and returns on timeout  
✓ **Timeout is not being set as `output_location` incorrectly** — status update works properly  
✓ **Worker loop isn't broken** — worker processes tasks, just gets stuck on this one  

---

### 5. LIKELY CAUSES OF THE HANG

Given the pattern (3 different worker IDs, same model, unusual resolution), the hang likely occurs **during WGP generation**:

### A. **Resolution-Related Issue** (Most Likely)
- **670×670 is unusual** — not a standard model grid multiple
- Different model versions may handle non-standard resolutions differently
- LTX2 i2v may have hard constraints on resolution compatibility
- Check: Is 670×670 grid-snapped to the model's grid size in `snap_resolution_to_model_grid()`?

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py`, lines 218-221:
```python
from source.utils.resolution_utils import get_model_grid_size
model_name = segment_params.get("model_name") or orchestrator_details.get("model_name")
grid_size = get_model_grid_size(model_name)
parsed_res_wh = snap_resolution_to_model_grid(parsed_res_raw, grid_size)
```

### B. **LTX2 Model Compatibility**
- Branch is `feat/ltx2-compatibility` — suggests recent LTX2 integration
- `wan_2_2_i2v_lightning_baseline_2_2_2` may have unresolved issues with:
  - Phase config (6 steps across 2 phases)
  - Image-to-video mode with unusual resolutions
  - Lightning model acceleration on this specific GPU

### C. **Image References Issue**
- Individual segment requires `orchestrator_details` with image paths
- If images don't load or are corrupted, WGP may hang waiting for them
- Check: Are the input images being downloaded successfully?

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py`, lines 273-499 (`_resolve_image_references()`)

### D. **Memory/Resource Exhaustion**
- 670×670 × 81 frames may cause OOM hang (not crash)
- Multiple workers on same GPU could deadlock each other
- Check: RAM/VRAM utilization during generation

---

### 6. DEBUG UTILITIES AVAILABLE

**Travel Debug Utils:**
- **File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/travel/debug_utils.py`
- **Functions:**
  - `debug_video_analysis()` — analyzes video files (frames, fps, duration, file size, color samples)
  - `log_ram_usage()` — logs process and system RAM metrics
- These are called in task processing to monitor resources

**Debug CLI:**
- **File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/debug/cli.py`
- Commands:
  - `debug task <task_id>` — investigate specific task
  - `debug worker <worker_id>` — investigate worker logs
  - `debug tasks` — analyze recent tasks with filtering
  - `debug health` — system health check

**Worker Debug Logging:**
- Worker logs all generation submissions with full parameters
- Check logs for: Did the task actually reach WGP? What were the resolved parameters?

---

### 7. HOW THE ERROR IS RECORDED

When timeout occurs (line 1094 in task_registry.py):

```
return False, f"Travel segment {task_id}: Generation timeout"
```

This flows through:
1. **task_registry.py** → returns `(False, error_message)` as tuple
2. **worker.py line 151** → unpacks as `generation_success, output_location_to_db`
3. **worker.py line 478** → calls `db_ops.update_task_status_supabase(current_task_id, STATUS_FAILED, output_location)`
4. **task_status.py line 98** → stores error_message in `output_location` field in DB

The error message `"Travel segment {task_id}: Generation timeout"` becomes the task's `output_location` in the database.

---

### 8. QUEUE PROCESSING DETAILS

**Task Flow:**
```
travel segment submitted to queue
    ↓
task_queue.submit_task(generation_task)  [line 1045]
    ↓
GenerationWorker thread picks it up
    ↓
process_task_impl() runs in worker thread [task_processor.py:42]
    ↓
execute_generation_impl() → WGP orchestrator
    ↓
[HANG/STALL FOR ~25 MINUTES]
    ↓
main thread in handle_travel_segment_via_queue() polls get_task_status()
    ↓
After 30 minutes: timeout
    ↓
Returns error to worker.py
    ↓
update_task_status_supabase() with error message
```

**Monitor Loop is NOT helping:**
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/queue/task_processor.py`, lines 491-504
- `_monitor_loop()` just sleeps 10 seconds with a placeholder comment: "future home for memory/queue/timeout monitoring"
- **No timeout detection or watchdog** at the WGP level

---

### 9. ROOT CAUSE SUMMARY

The timeout mechanism works correctly. The **real problem is WGP generation hangs**, likely due to:

1. **Resolution mismatch** — 670×670 may not be valid for the LTX2 i2v model
2. **Model compatibility** — LTX2 integration on this branch may have edge case bugs
3. **Phase config issues** — 6-step lightning config with 2 phases may conflict with model expectations
4. **Resource contention** — multiple workers fighting for GPU/memory

The fix is **NOT to change the 1800-second timeout**, but to:
1. **Investigate what causes WGP to hang** for ~25 minutes
2. **Add a watchdog timer** in the monitor loop to detect and kill stuck generations
3. **Validate resolution compatibility** before submission
4. **Test LTX2 i2v with 670×670 resolution** to confirm support

---

### 10. ABSOLUTE FILE PATHS

Key files for investigation:

- **Timeout logic:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py`
- **Task processor:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/queue/task_processor.py`
- **Worker main loop:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/worker.py`
- **Status updates:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/core/db/task_status.py`
- **Debug tools:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/travel/debug_utils.py`
- **Debug CLI:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/debug/cli.py`
- **Image resolution:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/utils/resolution_utils.py`

### What's Already on Main

The `banodoco/Reigh-Worker` main branch already has:
- The **full codebase refactor** (decomposed files, narrowed exceptions, structured logging, contracts, tests -- the 1817-test achievement)
- The **feat/ltx2-compatibility** branch merged (commit `81ab6fb Merge branch 'feat/ltx2-compatibility'`), which added LTX-2 model compatibility to the travel pipeline
- VACE detection fix (`9729c73`)
- The old `ltxv_13B.json` and `ltxv_distilled.json` Wan2GP defaults

Main does **NOT** have:
- The Wan2GP v10.83 upstream sync (no TTS models, no ace_step, no heartmula, no ltx2_19B defaults, no flux2 variants)
- Any of the IC LoRA pipeline routing
- The LTX-2 multi-frame guided video / latent injection work
- The union control LoRA support
- The `ltx2_ic_multiframe` task type
- The `apply_changes` -> `get_default_settings` API migration

---

### Key Finding: The PRs Only Diff Wan2GP Files + .gitignore + README

This is the critical observation. Despite the commit messages mentioning `source/` files like `task_conversion`, `orchestrator`, and `headless_wgp`, **the actual file diff only shows changes to `Wan2GP/*`, `.gitignore`, and `README.md`**. This means one of two things:

1. The fork's `source/` files diverged from `banodoco/Reigh-Worker` main before the refactor, and the merge conflicts brought those back into alignment -- so the diff against current main only shows the Wan2GP vendor changes.
2. The `source/` changes referenced in the commit messages were already incorporated into `banodoco/Reigh-Worker` main via other means (like the `feat/ltx2-compatibility` merge).

---

### Recommendations

1. **PR #16 is redundant.** It is a strict subset of PR #19. Every commit in PR #16 appears in PR #19. PR #16 can be **closed without merging**.

2. **PR #19 contains valuable, unmerged work**, specifically:
   - **Wan2GP v10.83 upstream sync** (50 new defaults, 37 new model files including TTS/HeartMula, TTS/ace_step). This is a significant vendor update.
   - **LTX-2 19B config files** (`ltx2_19B.json`, `ltx2_19B_nvfp4.json`, `ltx2_distilled.json`, plus GGUF quantized variants)
   - Minor `.gitignore` updates (6 additions, 3 deletions)
   - README changes (15 additions)

3. **The conflict situation:** Both PRs are CONFLICTING because they target the same files (the Wan2GP vendor tree) that have likely changed on main since the PRs were created. Since the diffs are entirely in the vendored `Wan2GP/` directory, the resolution approach would be:
   - Close PR #16
   - For PR #19, either rebase the fork against current main, or more practically, perform the Wan2GP v10.83 sync directly (since the `source/` integration work is already on main via the `feat/ltx2-compatibility` merge)

4. **Practical path forward:** Since the only actual value remaining in PR #19 is the **Wan2GP vendor directory update to v10.83**, the cleanest approach would be to update the vendored `Wan2GP/` directory directly from the upstream DeepBeepMeep/Wan2GP repository to the v10.83 tag/commit, rather than trying to resolve the merge conflicts in the fork-based PR.

Opus 4.6"}],"committedDate":"2026-02-13T23:44:29Z","messageBody":"Introduce model-aware parameterization so the travel pipeline supports\nboth Wan (grid=16, frame_step=4, fps=16) and LTX-2 (grid=64,\nframe_step=8, fps=24) without breaking existing behavior.\n\nKey changes:\n- resolution_utils: add get_model_grid_size(), parameterize grid snapping\n- orchestrator: replace hardcoded //4 quantization with _quantize_frames(frame_step),\n  add model-aware FPS helpers, auto-disable SVI for LTX-2\n- preflight: parameterize min_frames safety check (Wan=5, LTX-2=17)\n- segment_processor: add LTX-2 TSEV prompt type builder\n- stitch: model-aware FPS fallback, audio mux from segments\n- ffmpeg_ops: add video_has_audio() and mux_audio_from_segments()\n- task_registry: pass grid_size to snap_resolution_to_model_grid\n- lora_utils: add ltx2 lora cleanup path\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.6 <noreply@anthropic.com>","messageHeadline":"feat: add LTX-2 model compatibility across travel ltx2_multiframe_handler.py: task handler (download, upload, execute)\n- ltx2_multiframe_params.py: reference parameters\n- test_ltx2_multiframe_smoke.py: structure/wiring smoke tests\n- test_ltx2_multiframe_gpu.py: GPU integration tests\n\nModified:\n- comfy_utils.py: add upload_image() to ComfyUIClient\n- task_types.py: register ltx2_multiframe model mapping\n- task_registry.py: register handler in dispatch\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.6 <noreply@anthropic.com>","messageHeadline":"feat: add LTX-2 multi-frame guided video @@ -1,4 +1,254 @@
 # Changelog
+### January 1st 2026: WanGP v10.01, Happy New Year !
+
+- **Wan 2.2 i2v Stable Vision Infinity Pro 2**: SVI Pro 2 offers potentially unlimited Videos to Continue for i2v models. It will use either the Start frame as a Reference Image or you may provide an Anchor image to be used across all the windows or multiple Anchor Images one per Window.
+
+- **Wan 2.1 Alpha 2**: This new version of Alpha generates transparent videos with fine-grained alpha detail (hair, glow, smoke). 
+
+- **Qwen Image 2512**: This December release offers Enhanced Human Realism, Finer Natural Details & Improved Text Rendering.
+
+- **Wan NVP4**: *Light2xv nvfp4 support for Wan 2.1 i2v & t2v 1.3B*, you can now load nvfp4 (4 bits quantized file) in WanGP. These will make really a difference with RTX 50xx as they support natively scaled FP4 calculation. Other GPUs will get the pytorch fallback which is slower. This model can be useful for machines with low RAM but don't expect significant VRAM reduction of much faster speed for non RTX 50xx owners. You will need to install the Light2xv kernels.
+
+- **Nunckaku int4 & fp4 support for Qwen 2509 & Z Image**: int4 versions will work with most GPUs, fp4 will accelerate only RTX50xx. You will need to install the nunchaku kernels. See light2xv nvfp4 above, as the other comments apply here too.
+
+- **Z Image Control Net 2.1**: Control Net upgraded should work better. I have enabled as well inpainting for the control net.
+
+- **New Qwen Loras Accelerators Added**
+
+*Quantization Kernels Wheels for Windows / Python 3.10 / Pytorch 2.70:*
+- *Light2xv (WAN-FP4)*
+   ```
+  pip install https://github.com/deepbeepmeep/kernels/releases/download/WAN_NVP4/lightx2v_kernel-0.0.1-cp39-abi3-win_amd64.whl
+   ```
+
+- *Nunchaku*
+   ```
+  pip install https://github.com/deepbeepmeep/kernels/releases/download/Nunchaku/nunchaku-1.1.0+torch2.7-cp310-cp310-win_amd64.whl
+   ```
+   
+### December 23 2025: WanGP v9.92, Early Christmas
+
+- **SCAIL Preview**: enjoy this *Wan Animate*, *Steady Dancer* contender that can support multiple people. Thanks to its 3D positioning, it can take into account which parts of the body are hidden and which are not. 
+
+WanGP version has the following perks: 3D pose Preprocessing entirely rewritten to be fast,  and compatible with any pytorch version, very Low VRAM requirements for multicharacters, experimental long gen mode / sliding windows (SCAIL Preview doesnt  support officialy long gen yet)
+
+- **pi-Flux 2**: you don't use Flux 2 because you find it too slow ? You won't be able to use this excuse anymore: pi-Flux 2 is *4 steps distills* of the best image generator. It supports both image edition and text to image generation.
+
+- **Zandinksy v5** : for the video models collectors among you, you can try the Zandinsky model families, the 2B model quality is especially impressive given its small size
+
+- **Qwen Image Layered**: a new Qwen Image variant that lets you extract RGBA layers of your images so that  each layer can be edited separately
+
+- **Qwen Image Edit Plus 2511**: Qwen Image Edit Plus 2511 improves identity preservation (especially at 1080p) and integrates out of the box popular effects such as religthing and camera changes
+
+- **loras accelerator**: *loras accelerator* for *Wan 2.2 t2v* and *Wan 2.1 i2v* have been added (activable using the *Profile settings* as usual) 
+
+*update 9.91*: added Kandinsky 5 & Qwen Image Layered\
+*update 9.92*: added Qwen Image Edit Plus 2511
+
+### December 14 2025: WanGP v9.86, Simple Pleasures...
+
+These two features are going to change the life of many people:
+- **Pause Button**: ever had a urge to use your GPU for a very important task that can't wait (a game for instance ?), here comes your new friend the *Pause* button. Not only it will suspend the current gen in progress but it will free most of the VRAM used by WanGP (please note that the RAM by WanGP used wont be released). When you are done just click the *Resume* button to restart exactly from where you stopped.
+
+- **WanGP Headless**:  trouble running remotely WanGP or having some stability issues with Gradio or your Web Browser. This is all past thanks to *WanGP Headless* mode. Here is how it works : first make you shopping list of Video Gen using the classic WanGP gradio interface. When you are done, click the *Save Queue* button and quit WanGP.
+
+Then in your terminal window just write this:
+```bash
+python wgp.py --process my_queue.zip
+```
+With WanGP 9.82, you can also process settings file (.json file exported using th *Export Settings* button):
+```bash
+python wgp.py --process my_settings.json
+```
+Processing Settings can be useful to do some quick gen / testing if you don't need to provide source image files (otherwise you will need to fill the paths to Start Images, Ref Image, ...)
+
+- **Output Filename Customization**: in the *Misc* tab you can now customize how the file names of new Generation are created, for example:
+```
+{date(YYYY-MM-DD_HH-mm-ss)}_{seed}_{prompt(50)}, {num_inference_steps}
+```
+
+- **Hunyuan Video 1.5 i2v distilled** : for those in need of their daily dose of new models, added *Hunyuan Video 1.5 i2v Distilled* (official release) + Lora Accelerator extracted from it (to be used in future finetunes). Also added *Magcache* support (optimized for 20 steps) for Hunyuan Video 1.5.
+
+- **Wan-Move** : Another model specialized to control motion using a *Start Image* and *Trajectories*. According to the author's paper it is the best one. *Motion Designer* has been upgraded to generate also trajectories for *Wan-Move*.
+
+- **Z-Image Control Net v2** : This is an upgrade of Z-Image Control Net. It offers much better results but requires much more processing an VRAM. But don't panic yet, as it was VRAM optimized. It was not an easy trick as this one is complex. It has also Inpainting support,but I need more info to release this feature.
+
+*update 9.81*: added Hunyuan Video 1.5 i2v distilled + magcache\
+*update 9.82*: added Settings headless processing, output file customization, refactored Task edition and queue processing\
+*update 9.83*: Qwen Edit+ upgraded: no more any zoom out at 1080p, enabled mask, enabled image refs with inpainting\
+*update 9.84*: added Wan-Move support\
+*update 9.85*: added Z-Image Control net v2\
+*update 9.86*: added NAG support for Z-Image
+
+### December 4 2025: WanGP v9.74, The Alpha & the Omega ... and the Dancer
+
+- **Flux 2**: the best ever open source *Image Generator* has just landed. It does everything very well: generate an *Image* based a *Text Prompt* or combine up to 10 *Images References* 
+
+The only snag is that it is a 60B parameters for the *Transformer* part and 40B parameters for the *Text Encoder* part.
+
+Behold the WanGP Miracle ! Flux 2 wil work with only 8 GB of VRAM if you are happy with 8 bits quantization (no need for lower quality 4bits). With 9GB of VRAM you can run the model at full power. You will need at least 64 GB of RAM. If not maybe Memory Profile 5 will be your friend.
+
+*With WanGP v9.74*, **Flux 2 Control Net** hidden power has also been unleashed from the vanilla model. You can now enjoy Flux 2 *Inpainting* and *Pose transfer*. This can be combined with *Image Refs* to get the best *Identity Preservation* / *Face Swapping* an Image Model can offer: just target the effect to a specific area using a *Mask* and set *Denoising Strength* to 0.9-1.0 and *Masking Strength* to 0.3-0.4 for a perfect blending 
+
+- **Z-Image**: a small model, very fast (8 steps), very low VRAM (optimized even more in WanGP for fun, just in case you want to generate 16 images at a time) that produces outstanding Image quality. Not yet the Flux 2 level, and no Image editing yet but a very good trade-off.
+
+While waiting for Z-Image edit, *WanGP 9.74* offers now support for **Z-Image Fun Control Net**. You can use it for *Pose transfer*, *Canny Edge* transfer. Don't be surprised if it is a bit slower. Please note it will work best at 1080p and will require a minimum of 9 steps.
+
+- **Steady Dancer**: here is *Wan Steady Dancer* a very nice alternative to *Wan Animate*. You can transfer the motion of a Control video in a very smooth way. It will work best with Videos where the action happens center stage (hint: *dancing*). Use the *Lora accelerator* *Fusionix i2v 10 steps* for a fast generation. For higher quality you can set *Condition Guidance* to 2 or if you are very patient keep *Guidance* to a value greater than 1.   
+
+I have added a new Memory Profile *Profile 4+* that is sligthly slower than *Profile 4* but can save you up to 1GB of VRAM with Flux 2.
```

+def optimized_scale(positive_flat, negative_flat):
+
+    # Calculate dot production
+    dot_product = torch.sum(positive_flat * negative_flat, dim=1, keepdim=True)
+
+    # Squared norm of uncondition
+    squared_norm = torch.sum(negative_flat ** 2, dim=1, keepdim=True) + 1e-8
+
+    # st_star = v_cond^T * v_uncond / ||v_uncond||^2
+    st_star = dot_product / squared_norm
+    
+    return st_star
+
+def timestep_transform(t, shift=5.0, num_timesteps=1000 ):
+    t = t / num_timesteps
+    # shift the timestep based on ratio
+    new_t = shift * t / (1 + (shift - 1) * t)
+    new_t = new_t * num_timesteps
+    return new_t
+    
+

@@ -1,4 +1,254 @@
 # Changelog
+### January 1st 2026: WanGP v10.01, Happy New Year !
+
+- **Wan 2.2 i2v Stable Vision Infinity Pro 2**: SVI Pro 2 offers potentially unlimited Videos to Continue for i2v models. It will use either the Start frame as a Reference Image or you may provide an Anchor image to be used across all the windows or multiple Anchor Images one per Window.
+
+- **Wan 2.1 Alpha 2**: This new version of Alpha generates transparent videos with fine-grained alpha detail (hair, glow, smoke). 
+
+- **Qwen Image 2512**: This December release offers Enhanced Human Realism, Finer Natural Details & Improved Text Rendering.
+
+- **Wan NVP4**: *Light2xv nvfp4 support for Wan 2.1 i2v & t2v 1.3B*, you can now load nvfp4 (4 bits quantized file) in WanGP. These will make really a difference with RTX 50xx as they support natively scaled FP4 calculation. Other GPUs will get the pytorch fallback which is slower. This model can be useful for machines with low RAM but don't expect significant VRAM reduction of much faster speed for non RTX 50xx owners. You will need to install the Light2xv kernels.
+
+- **Nunckaku int4 & fp4 support for Qwen 2509 & Z Image**: int4 versions will work with most GPUs, fp4 will accelerate only RTX50xx. You will need to install the nunchaku kernels. See light2xv nvfp4 above, as the other comments apply here too.
+
+- **Z Image Control Net 2.1**: Control Net upgraded should work better. I have enabled as well inpainting for the control net.
+
+- **New Qwen Loras Accelerators Added**
+
+*Quantization Kernels Wheels for Windows / Python 3.10 / Pytorch 2.70:*
+- *Light2xv (WAN-FP4)*
+   ```
+  pip install https://github.com/deepbeepmeep/kernels/releases/download/WAN_NVP4/lightx2v_kernel-0.0.1-cp39-abi3-win_amd64.whl
+   ```
+
+- *Nunchaku*
+   ```
+  pip install https://github.com/deepbeepmeep/kernels/releases/download/Nunchaku/nunchaku-1.1.0+torch2.7-cp310-cp310-win_amd64.whl
+   ```
+   
+### December 23 2025: WanGP v9.92, Early Christmas

tool_result Bash
```
# Changelog
### January 1st 2026: WanGP v10.01, Happy New Year !

- **Wan 2.2 i2v Stable Vision Infinity Pro 2**: SVI Pro 2 offers potentially unlimited Videos to Continue for i2v models. It will use either the Start frame as a Reference Image or you may provide an Anchor image to be used across all the windows or multiple Anchor Images one per Window.

- **Wan 2.1 Alpha 2**: This new version of Alpha generates transparent videos with fine-grained alpha detail (hair, glow, smoke). 

- **Qwen Image 2512**: This December release offers Enhanced Human Realism, Finer Natural Details & Improved Text Rendering.

- **Wan NVP4**: *Light2xv nvfp4 support for Wan 2.1 i2v & t2v 1.3B*, you can now load nvfp4 (4 bits quantized file) in WanGP. These will make really a difference with RTX 50xx as they support natively scaled FP4 calculation. Other GPUs will get the pytorch fallback which is slower. This model can be useful for machines with low RAM but don't expect significant VRAM reduction of much faster speed for non RTX 50xx owners. You will need to install the Light2xv kernels.

- **Nunckaku int4 & fp4 support for Qwen 2509 & Z Image**: int4 versions will work with most GPUs, fp4 will accelerate only RTX50xx. You will need to install the nunchaku kernels. See light2xv nvfp4 above, as the other comments apply here too.

- **Z Image Control Net 2.1**: Control Net upgraded should work better. I have enabled as well inpainting for the control net.

- **New Qwen Loras Accelerators Added**

*Quantization Kernels Wheels for Windows / Python 3.10 / Pytorch 2.70:*
- *Light2xv (WAN-FP4)*
   ```
  pip install https://github.com/deepbeepmeep/kernels/releases/download/WAN_NVP4/lightx2v_kernel-0.0.1-cp39-abi3-win_amd64.whl
   ```

- *Nunchaku*
   ```
  pip install https://github.com/deepbeepmeep/kernels/releases/download/Nunchaku/nunchaku-1.1.0+torch2.7-cp310-cp310-win_amd64.whl
   ```
   
### December 23 2025: WanGP v9.92, Early Christmas

- **SCAIL Preview**: enjoy this *Wan Animate*, *Steady Dancer* contender that can support multiple people. Thanks to its 3D positioning, it can take into account which parts of the body are hidden and which are not. 

WanGP version has the following perks: 3D pose Preprocessing entirely rewritten to be fast,  and compatible with any pytorch version, very Low VRAM requirements for multicharacters, experimental long gen mode / sliding windows (SCAIL Preview doesnt  support officialy long gen yet)

- **pi-Flux 2**: you don't use Flux 2 because you find it too slow ? You won't be able to use this excuse anymore: pi-Flux 2 is *4 steps distills* of the best image generator. It supports both image edition and text to image generation.

- **Zandinksy v5** : for the video models collectors among you, you can try the Zandinsky model families, the 2B model quality is especially impressive given its small size

- **Qwen Image Layered**: a new Qwen Image variant that lets you extract RGBA layers of your images so that  each layer can be edited separately

- **Qwen Image Edit Plus 2511**: Qwen Image Edit Plus 2511 improves identity preservation (especially at 1080p) and integrates out of the box popular effects such as religthing and camera changes

- **loras accelerator**: *loras accelerator* for *Wan 2.2 t2v* and *Wan 2.1 i2v* have been added (activable using the *Profile settings* as usual) 

*update 9.91*: added Kandinsky 5 & Qwen Image Layered\
*update 9.92*: added Qwen Image Edit Plus 2511

### December 14 2025: WanGP v9.86, Simple Pleasures...

These two features are going to change the life of many people:
- **Pause Button**: ever had a urge to use your GPU for a very important task that can't wait (a game for instance ?), here comes your new friend the *Pause* button. Not only it will suspend the current gen in progress but it will free most of the VRAM used by WanGP (please note that the RAM by WanGP used wont be released). When you are done just click the *Resume* button to restart exactly from where you stopped.

- **WanGP Headless**:  trouble running remotely WanGP or having some stability issues with Gradio or your Web Browser. This is all past thanks to *WanGP Headless* mode. Here is how it works : first make you shopping list of Video Gen using the classic WanGP gradio interface. When you are done, click the *Save Queue* button and quit WanGP.

Then in your terminal window just write this:
```bash
python wgp.py --process my_queue.zip
```
With WanGP 9.82, you can also process settings file (.json file exported using th *Export Settings* button):
```bash
python wgp.py --process my_settings.json
```
Processing Settings can be useful to do some quick gen / testing if you don't need to provide source image files (otherwise you will need to fill the paths to Start Images, Ref Image, ...)

- **Output Filename Customization**: in the *Misc* tab you can now customize how the file names of new Generation are created, for example:
```
{date(YYYY-MM-DD_HH-mm-ss)}_{seed}_{prompt(50)}, {num_inference_steps}
```

- **Hunyuan Video 1.5 i2v distilled** : for those in need of their daily dose of new models, added *Hunyuan Video 1.5 i2v Distilled* (official release) + Lora Accelerator extracted from it (to be used in future finetunes). Also added *Magcache* support (optimized for 20 steps) for Hunyuan Video 1.5.

- **Wan-Move** : Another model specialized to control motion using a *Start Image* and *Trajectories*. According to the author's paper it is the best one. *Motion Designer* has been upgraded to generate also trajectories for *Wan-Move*.

- **Z-Image Control Net v2** : This is an upgrade of Z-Image Control Net. It offers much better results but requires much more processing an VRAM. But don't panic yet, as it was VRAM optimized. It was not an easy trick as this one is complex. It has also Inpainting support,but I need more info to release this feature.

*update 9.81*: added Hunyuan Video 1.5 i2v distilled + magcache\
*update 9.82*: added Settings headless processing, output file customization, refactored Task edition and queue processing\
*update 9.83*: Qwen Edit+ upgraded: no more any zoom out at 1080p, enabled mask, enabled image refs with inpainting\
*update 9.84*: added Wan-Move support\
*update 9.85*: added Z-Image Control net v2\
```

     8→- **Ready to use Loras Accelerators Profiles** per type of model that you can apply on your current *Generation Settings*. Next time I will recommend a *Lora Accelerator*, it will be only one click away. And best of all of the required Loras will be downloaded automatically. When you apply an *Accelerator Profile*, input fields like the *Number of Denoising Steps* *Activated Loras*, *Loras Multipliers* (such as "1;0 0;1" ...) will be automatically filled. However your video specific fields will be preserved, so it will be easy to switch between Profiles to experiment. With *WanGP 8.993*, the *Accelerator Loras* are now merged with *Non Accelerator Loras". Things are getting too easy...
     9→
    10→- **Embedded Loras URL** : WanGP will now try to remember every Lora URLs it sees. For instance if someone sends you some settings that contain Loras URLs or you extract the Settings of Video generated by a friend with Loras URLs, these URLs will be automatically added to *WanGP URL Cache*. Conversely everything you will share (Videos, Settings, Lset files) will contain the download URLs if they are known. You can also download directly a Lora in WanGP by using the *Download Lora* button a the bottom. The Lora will be immediatly available and added to WanGP lora URL cache. This will work with *Hugging Face* as a repository. Support for CivitAi will come as soon as someone will nice enough to post a GitHub PR ...

## Summary: Wan2GP Comparison -- Current Main vs PR #19

### Version Information

| Property | Current Main | PR #19 | Upstream (deepbeepmeep) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `WanGP_version` | **10.01** | **10.83** | **10.9** |
| `target_mmgp_version` | 3.6.10 | 3.7.4 | 3.7.5 |
| `settings_version` | 2.42 | 2.50 | 2.52 |

**The PR's Wan2GP is significantly newer** -- it jumps 82 minor versions from v10.01 to v10.83. However, even the PR is slightly behind the latest upstream (v10.9).

---

### What PR #19 Changes in Wan2GP/ (447 files total)

**By change type:**
- **275 files added** -- Mostly new model families (TTS: HeartMula, AceStep, AceStep15, KugelAudio, Qwen3 TTS, Chatterbox handlers) and new LTX-2 model configs
- **100 files modified** -- Core engine files like `wgp.py`, `any2video.py`, model handlers, and shared utilities
- **51 files renamed** -- Chatterbox TTS reorganization (moved from `models/chatterbox/` to `models/TTS/chatterbox/`)
- **21 files removed** -- See critical removals below

---

### Critical Removals (21 files)

**Default configs removed (13 files):**
- `wan_2_2_i2v_lightning_baseline_2_2_2.json`
- `wan_2_2_i2v_lightning_baseline_3_3.json`
- `wan_2_2_i2v_lightning_svi_3_3.json`
- `wan_2_2_i2v_lightning_svi_endframe.json`
- `wan_2_2_vace_lightning_baseline_2_2_2.json`
- `wan_2_2_vace_lightning_baseline_3_3.json`
- `vace_fun_14B_cocktail_lightning.json` (+ 3phase, 3phase_backup, 3phase_light_distill)
- `vace_14B_fake_cocktail_2_2.json`
- `optimised-t2i.json`
- `qwen_image_hires.json`
- `z_image_img2img.json`

**Uni3C module removed (3 files):**
- `Wan2GP/models/wan/uni3c/__init__.py`
- `Wan2GP/models/wan/uni3c/controlnet.py`
- `Wan2GP/models/wan/uni3c/load.py`

**Other removals:**
- `Wan2GP/extract_source_images.py`
- `Wan2GP/finetunes/qwen_image_edit_20B.json`
- `Wan2GP/models/chatterbox/chatterbox_handler.py` (moved to TTS/)
- `Wan2GP/models/flux/modules/layers copy.py` (cleanup of duplicate file)

**Default configs added (notable):**
- `ltx2_19B.json`, `ltx2_19B_nvfp4.json`, `ltx2_distilled.json` + GGUF variants
- `ace_step_v1.json`, `ace_step_v1_5*.json` (music generation)
- `flux2_klein_*.json`, `flux2_dev_nvfp4.json`
- `heartmula_*.json`, `kugelaudio_0_open.json`
- `qwen3_tts_*.json`
- `z_image_base.json`, `z_image_twinflow_turbo.json`

---

### RISKS -- Custom Modifications That Would Be Lost

#### RISK 1 (HIGH): Uni3C ControlNet Integration -- WILL BREAK

The PR **removes** the entire `Wan2GP/models/wan/uni3c/` directory (3 files). Our codebase actively imports from this module:

- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/queue/memory_cleanup.py` line 42: `from Wan2GP.models.wan.uni3c import clear_uni3c_cache_if_unused`
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/models/wgp/model_ops.py` line 228: `from models.wan.uni3c import load_uni3c_controlnet`

Additionally, **14 source files** reference uni3c in various capacities (parameters, config, processing).

#### RISK 2 (HIGH): Custom any2video.py Uni3C Code -- WILL BE LOST

Our local `any2video.py` is **2,222 lines** with **53 references to uni3c**. The PR's version is only **1,444 lines** with **zero uni3c references**. Our custom additions include:
- `_load_uni3c_guide_video()` method
- `_apply_uni3c_frame_policy()` method
- `_encode_uni3c_guide()` method
- ~10 uni3c parameters in `generate_video()` (use_uni3c, uni3c_guide_video, uni3c_strength, etc.)
- Full uni3c denoising integration in the sampling loop

Neither the upstream WanGP nor the PR's version contain any uni3c code -- this is entirely our custom integration.

#### RISK 3 (MEDIUM): Removed Default Configs Still Referenced

Our codebase hardcodes references to configs that the PR removes:
- `wan_2_2_vace_lightning_baseline_2_2_2` is the **default model** for `join_clips_segment` and `inpaint_frames` task types (referenced in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/tasks/task_types.py` lines 92-93)
- Used as default in travel orchestrator, join generation, and inpaint_frames handlers
- `z_image_img2img` is referenced in task_conversion.py line 245 as a model type
- `qwen_image_hires` has a dedicated handler in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/models/model_handlers/qwen_handler.py`

#### RISK 4 (MEDIUM): wgp.py API Changes

The PR modifies wgp.py heavily (+1741/-811 lines). Per the commit messages, v10.83 **removed the `apply_changes()` function** -- config is now applied at module level during import. The PR already addresses this with `get_default_settings` + `set_model_settings`. Our orchestrator at `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/models/wgp/orchestrator.py` would need the same adaptation.

#### RISK 5 (LOW): Non-Wan2GP Source Changes in PR

The PR also modifies 18 files under `source/` including:
- `source/models/wgp/orchestrator.py` -- LTX-2 support, apply_changes removal
- `source/models/wgp/generators/wgp_params.py` -- 12 new v10.x parameters
- `source/task_handlers/tasks/task_types.py` -- LTX-2 model mapping
- `source/task_handlers/travel/` -- LTX-2 model-aware travel pipeline
- `source/utils/resolution_utils.py` -- parameterized grid snapping

These would conflict with our decomposed/refactored codebase since the PR was built against a different code organization.

---

### Bottom Line

1. **Yes, the PR's Wan2GP is significantly newer** (v10.83 vs v10.01), spanning ~82 upstream versions with major new features: LTX-2 19B, Flux 2 Klein, AceStep TTS, HeartMula TTS, KugelAudio, Qwen3 TTS, and many model improvements.

2. **The biggest risk is our Uni3C ControlNet integration.** We have ~800 lines of custom code in `any2video.py` and a separate `uni3c/` module package, all of which the PR would destroy. This would need to be re-applied on top of the v10.83 any2video.py.

3. **Several default configs we hardcode as defaults are deleted** in v10.83. These would need replacement model names or the configs preserved locally.

4. **The wgp.py API changed** (apply_changes removed), requiring orchestrator adaptation.

5. **The PR is already slightly behind upstream** (v10.83 vs v10.9), so a direct sync to upstream v10.9 might be preferable to taking the PR's version.
